# Unit 28 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 28 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 28 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

`unitContentReviewDigest` binds this unit's instructional curriculum. It covers this unit and the reachable instructional records, relationships, shared dependencies, sources, rights, assets, and advancement rule. It does not cover any other unit. Recording a review status, reviewer signature, mapping approval, install state, or readiness does not change it.

`mappingPackageDigest` binds the proposed edition-mapping facts for Unit 28: edition identity, lesson, printed page range, topic description, observation source, ISBN observation state, and the pending disposition. Reviewer identity, qualification, date, notes, correction notes, and signature stay outside that digest.

`packContentReviewDigest` is the repository aggregate of instructional curriculum. It is not an approval of this unit, and it is not part of the review-package digest.

`reviewPackageDigest` identifies this complete pending review package. It binds the ordered instructional records and the mapping-package digest. Changing a proposed page range or topic changes the mapping-package digest and therefore this package digest.

`repositorySourceDigest` binds repository and release inputs. Recording an approval later changes that repository hash. That change does not replace the signed content-review digest or the mapping-package digest.

The marker scan used by this package detects only a fixed list of known prohibited mapping-input markers. It is not comprehensive copyright detection and cannot recognize every copy or close paraphrase of protected material.

language=en
prompt=Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
promptLetter=читая письмо, он улыбался
options=simultaneous with the main predicate | an absolute present label | a different subject from the main verb
answer=simultaneous with the main predicate
feedback=In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.

containsGeneratedRussian=false
reviewStatus=IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT

independent=false

whyAnswerDemonstratesObjective=In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
contrastMembers=simultaneous with the main predicate | an absolute present label | a different subject from the main verb

detail=Wikipedia article text is frozen by oldid under CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikipedia:Copyrights oldid 1369466282. The Wikibooks address note is frozen by oldid under the separate CC BY-SA 4.0 route recorded on Wikibooks:Copyrights oldid 4622060. The packaged CC BY-SA 4.0 legal code is cc-by-sa-4.0.txt. The adapted selection and arrangement are share-alike. Application code, user data, user recordings, and separately licensed audio are outside that statement. No audio is included. Linked media, Phonos, and pronunciation files are not admitted. Anecdotal postal advice is not shipped. Russian Wikipedia morphology oldid 148939075 is admitted only for the abbreviation declension contrast and the command-versus-supposition particle contrast, under the Russian Wikipedia copyrights oldid 154562152. Russian Wikipedia indefinite-pronoun page oldid 149963314 is admitted for the series meanings under that same Russian Wikipedia copyrights route. Russian Wikipedia participle page oldid 153078996 is admitted for the participial-phrase punctuation rule, including its exceptions, under that same route. Complete postal practice, participle stress, a general bookish-register claim, and dictionary use are not prepared. ReadyRussian letter conventions cover structure, the Russian and English organizational difference, and the stranger address limit. The abbreviation contrast and the particle contrast do not activate an authority variance. Review is pending. Bounded postal practice, past-passive stress, and a two-entry dictionary task are implemented and review-pending. They are not complete postal mastery, a general stress rule, or broad dictionary proficiency. Present-passive exploration does not gate Unit 27.

написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
написав is anterior to отправит. The main action is future, so the verbal adverb is not an absolute past.
A perfective verbal adverb is anterior to its main predicate, including a future predicate.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.

написав письмо
она отправит его завтра
написав
отправит.
отправит

читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label ignores the past main verb; the verbal adverb has relative time.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject condition in this construction.
читая письмо,
читая письмо
он улыбался
читая
он улыбался,

читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label ignores the past main verb; the verbal adverb has relative time.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject condition in this construction.

читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label ignores the past main verb; the verbal adverb has relative time.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject condition in this construction.

читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.

написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.

Bibliography includes the Gramota dictionary entries for некто and быть, the Gramota statement of vowels after hushers and ц, and Litnevskaya on the verbal adverb as search evidence for relative time. An imperfective form can report a completed event without asserting completion. Authorship: independently authored for Russian Companion. Status remains IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_FINAL_AUDIT. This note is not linguistic acceptance, mapping approval, installation, or owner acceptance. No owned-book prose, exercise, or answer key is copied.
написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.

recordType=original-synthesis
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-corrected-capability:cap-obj-u28-imperfective-adverb
originalSourceText=Pending corrected research applications. No linguistic or owner approval is recorded.
References: retained original source bindings in this unit; the independent content-audit register; Gramota dictionary and orthography; Litnevskaya, verbal adverbs; Rusgram, verbal adverbs.
https://gramota.ru/poisk?query=быстрый&mode=slovari
https://gramota.ru/biblioteka/spravochniki/russkij-yazyk-kratkij-teoreticheskij-kurs-dlya-shkolnikov/deeprichastie
Proposed Unit 28 uses admitted imperfective and perfective adverbial lines and one admitted public-domain reading span. Qualified review is pending.
Read the admitted examples on this card.
An imperfective verbal adverb is simultaneous with the main predicate. It has no independent tense, so it is not usually present as an absolute label. The subject is the same as the main verb.
imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
думая
imperfective verbal adverb; relative time is interpreted with its main predicate, normally simultaneous in the taught construction
учась
читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label ignores the past main verb; the verbal adverb has relative time.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject condition in this construction.
the reader and the person smiling are the same person
Which English description identifies the shared subject?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, the reader and the person smiling are the same person: the verbal adverb shares the subject of the main predicate.
two unrelated people perform the actions
the sentence omits its main subject
two unrelated people perform the actions is not the shared-subject construction shown here.
the sentence omits its main subject is incorrect because the displayed main clause has an explicit subject.

Proposed Unit 28 uses admitted imperfective and perfective adverbial lines and one admitted public-domain reading span. Qualified review is pending.
Read the admitted examples on this card.
A perfective verbal adverb is anterior to the main predicate. It can precede a past or a future main action. It is not limited to an absolute past.
usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
сказав
perfective verbal adverb; normally anterior to its main action, which can itself be past or future
научившись
войдя
написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.
the writer and sender are the same person
Which English description identifies the shared subject?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, the writer and sender are the same person: the verbal adverb shares the subject of the main predicate.
two unrelated people perform the actions
the sentence omits its main subject
two unrelated people perform the actions is not the shared-subject construction shown here.
the sentence omits its main subject is incorrect because the displayed main clause has an explicit subject.

A perfective verbal adverb is anterior to the main predicate. It can precede a past or a future main action. It is not limited to an absolute past.
usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
сказав
perfective verbal adverb; normally anterior to its main action, which can itself be past or future
научившись
войдя
написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.
the writer and sender are the same person

Proposed Unit 28 uses admitted imperfective and perfective adverbial lines and one admitted public-domain reading span. Qualified review is pending.
Read the admitted examples on this card.
A perfective verbal adverb is anterior to the main predicate. It can precede a past or a future main action. It is not limited to an absolute past.
usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
сказав
perfective verbal adverb; normally anterior to its main action, which can itself be past or future
научившись
войдя
написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.
the writer and sender are the same person
Which English description identifies the shared subject?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, the writer and sender are the same person: the verbal adverb shares the subject of the main predicate.
two unrelated people perform the actions
the sentence omits its main subject
two unrelated people perform the actions is not the shared-subject construction shown here.
the sentence omits its main subject is incorrect because the displayed main clause has an explicit subject.

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (s
originalSourceText=imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (since they denote action performed by the subject, the tense corresponds to the time of action denoted by the verb). Adverbial participles are usually active, but passive constructions may be formed using adverbial participle forms of the verb [[wikt:быть|быть]] (present ''будучи'' "being", very rarely past ''бывши'' "having been"); these may be combined with either an adjectival participle in the [[instrumental case]] (Будучи [[wikt:ранить|раненным]], боец оставался в строю – Being wounded, the combatant remained in the row), or a short adjective in the nominative (Бывши один раз наказан, он больше так не делал – Having been punished once, he didn't do it any more).

Present adverbial participles are formed by adding the suffix -а/-я (or sometimes -учи/-ючи, which is usually deprecated) to the stem of the present tense. A few past adverbial participles (mainly of intransitive verbs of motion) are formed in the same way, but most are formed with the suffix -в (alternative form -вши, always used before -сь), some whose stem ends with a consonant, with -ши. For reflexive verbs, the suffix -сь remains at the very end of the word; in poetry it can take the form -ся.<ref>{{Cite book|pages=162, 164|title=Russian: A Linguistic Introduction|publisher=Cambridge University Press|author=Paul Cubberley|url={{google book|plainurl=y|id=yOGzAyN3V88C|page=10}}|isbn=0-521-79641-5|year=2002}}</ref><ref>{{Cite book|pages=180|title=Современный русский язык. Морфология|url={{google books |plainurl=y |id=GA8CMwEACAAJ}}|publisher=Издательство МГУ|author=А. А. Камынина|year=1999|isbn=5-211-04133-X}}</ref>

In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk"). But in some conservative dialects, adverbial and adjectival participles may be used to produce [[Perfect (grammar)|perfect]] forms, which do not occur in standard Russian; e.g. "I haven't eaten today" will be "Я сегодня не евши" instead of "Я сегодня не ел".

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (since they denote actio
originalSourceText=usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (since they denote action performed by the subject, the tense corresponds to the time of action denoted by the verb). Adverbial participles are usually active, but passive constructions may be formed using adverbial participle forms of the verb [[wikt:быть|быть]] (present ''будучи'' "being", very rarely past ''бывши'' "having been"); these may be combined with either an adjectival participle in the [[instrumental case]] (Будучи [[wikt:ранить|раненным]], боец оставался в строю – Being wounded, the combatant remained in the row), or a short adjective in the nominative (Бывши один раз наказан, он больше так не делал – Having been punished once, he didn't do it any more).

Present adverbial participles are formed by adding the suffix -а/-я (or sometimes -учи/-ючи, which is usually deprecated) to the stem of the present tense. A few past adverbial participles (mainly of intransitive verbs of motion) are formed in the same way, but most are formed with the suffix -в (alternative form -вши, always used before -сь), some whose stem ends with a consonant, with -ши. For reflexive verbs, the suffix -сь remains at the very end of the word; in poetry it can take the form -ся.<ref>{{Cite book|pages=162, 164|title=Russian: A Linguistic Introduction|publisher=Cambridge University Press|author=Paul Cubberley|url={{google book|plainurl=y|id=yOGzAyN3V88C|page=10}}|isbn=0-521-79641-5|year=2002}}</ref><ref>{{Cite book|pages=180|title=Современный русский язык. Морфология|url={{google books |plainurl=y |id=GA8CMwEACAAJ}}|publisher=Издательство МГУ|author=А. А. Камынина|year=1999|isbn=5-211-04133-X}}</ref>

In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk"). But in some conservative dialects, adverbial and adjectival participles may be used to produce [[Perfect (grammar)|perfect]] forms, which do not occur in standard Russian; e.g. "I haven't eaten today" will be "Я сегодня не евши" instead of "Я сегодня не ел".

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://ru.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Вечера_у_княгини_Старобельской_(Чернышевский)&oldid=5336197
sourceRevision=5336197
sourceRecordId=vechera:устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и уры
originalSourceText=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и урывками между этих занятий услышал утром голос старушки, хозяйки домика: «самовар принесла, Павел Сергеевич», сказал ей «благодарю, Анна Даниловна», выпил два стакана чаю с молоком, съел с чаем ломоть белого хлеба; опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла завтрак, Павел Сергеевич», опять поблагодарил ее, выпил стакан молока с черным хлебом, составлявший его завтрак, опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла обед, Павел Сергеич», опять поблагодарил ее, съел тарелку грешневой кашицы, составлявшую его ежедневный обед; опять услышал — это было, наверное часов в 6, по аккуратно соблюдаемому старушкой порядку, — голос ее «принесла самовар, Павел Сергеич!», опять сказал «благодарю вас, Анна Даниловна», — но тут произошло отступление от ежедневного порядка: встав положить чаю в чайник, и налить кипятком, Павел Сергеич увидел, что старушка не ушла, стоит, сложа руки
shippedText=ложился на диван

recordType=example
sourceUrl=https://ru.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Вечера_у_княгини_Старобельской_(Чернышевский)&oldid=5336197

originalSourceText=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и урывками между этих занятий услышал утром голос старушки, хозяйки домика: «самовар принесла, Павел Сергеевич», сказал ей «благодарю, Анна Даниловна», выпил два стакана чаю с молоком, съел с чаем ломоть белого хлеба; опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла завтрак, Павел Сергеевич», опять поблагодарил ее, выпил стакан молока с черным хлебом, составлявший его завтрак, опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла обед, Павел Сергеич», опять поблагодарил ее, съел тарелку грешневой кашицы, составлявшую его ежедневный обед; опять услышал — это было, наверное часов в 6, по аккуратно соблюдаемому старушкой порядку, — голос ее «принесла самовар, Павел Сергеич!», опять сказал «благодарю вас, Анна Даниловна», — но тут произошло отступление от ежедневного порядка: встав положить чаю в чайник, и налить кипятком, Павел Сергеич увидел, что старушка не ушла, стоит, сложа руки

- Content hash: 5cf113b7556bc5f4ee037fa7bb69d52f9debef2f063a9a1f661d1eba989a1a68

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (s
originalSourceText=imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (since they denote action performed by the subject, the tense corresponds to the time of action denoted by the verb). Adverbial participles are usually active, but passive constructions may be formed using adverbial participle forms of the verb [[wikt:быть|быть]] (present ''будучи'' "being", very rarely past ''бывши'' "having been"); these may be combined with either an adjectival participle in the [[instrumental case]] (Будучи [[wikt:ранить|раненным]], боец оставался в строю – Being wounded, the combatant remained in the row), or a short adjective in the nominative (Бывши один раз наказан, он больше так не делал – Having been punished once, he didn't do it any more).

Present adverbial participles are formed by adding the suffix -а/-я (or sometimes -учи/-ючи, which is usually deprecated) to the stem of the present tense. A few past adverbial participles (mainly of intransitive verbs of motion) are formed in the same way, but most are formed with the suffix -в (alternative form -вши, always used before -сь), some whose stem ends with a consonant, with -ши. For reflexive verbs, the suffix -сь remains at the very end of the word; in poetry it can take the form -ся.<ref>{{Cite book|pages=162, 164|title=Russian: A Linguistic Introduction|publisher=Cambridge University Press|author=Paul Cubberley|url={{google book|plainurl=y|id=yOGzAyN3V88C|page=10}}|isbn=0-521-79641-5|year=2002}}</ref><ref>{{Cite book|pages=180|title=Современный русский язык. Морфология|url={{google books |plainurl=y |id=GA8CMwEACAAJ}}|publisher=Издательство МГУ|author=А. А. Камынина|year=1999|isbn=5-211-04133-X}}</ref>

In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk"). But in some conservative dialects, adverbial and adjectival participles may be used to produce [[Perfect (grammar)|perfect]] forms, which do not occur in standard Russian; e.g. "I haven't eaten today" will be "Я сегодня не евши" instead of "Я сегодня не ел".

| думая

| сказав (сказавши)
|-

recordType=label
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (s
originalSourceText=imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past (since they denote action performed by the subject, the tense corresponds to the time of action denoted by the verb). Adverbial participles are usually active, but passive constructions may be formed using adverbial participle forms of the verb [[wikt:быть|быть]] (present ''будучи'' "being", very rarely past ''бывши'' "having been"); these may be combined with either an adjectival participle in the [[instrumental case]] (Будучи [[wikt:ранить|раненным]], боец оставался в строю – Being wounded, the combatant remained in the row), or a short adjective in the nominative (Бывши один раз наказан, он больше так не делал – Having been punished once, he didn't do it any more).

Present adverbial participles are formed by adding the suffix -а/-я (or sometimes -учи/-ючи, which is usually deprecated) to the stem of the present tense. A few past adverbial participles (mainly of intransitive verbs of motion) are formed in the same way, but most are formed with the suffix -в (alternative form -вши, always used before -сь), some whose stem ends with a consonant, with -ши. For reflexive verbs, the suffix -сь remains at the very end of the word; in poetry it can take the form -ся.<ref>{{Cite book|pages=162, 164|title=Russian: A Linguistic Introduction|publisher=Cambridge University Press|author=Paul Cubberley|url={{google book|plainurl=y|id=yOGzAyN3V88C|page=10}}|isbn=0-521-79641-5|year=2002}}</ref><ref>{{Cite book|pages=180|title=Современный русский язык. Морфология|url={{google books |plainurl=y |id=GA8CMwEACAAJ}}|publisher=Издательство МГУ|author=А. А. Камынина|year=1999|isbn=5-211-04133-X}}</ref>

In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk"). But in some conservative dialects, adverbial and adjectival participles may be used to produce [[Perfect (grammar)|perfect]] forms, which do not occur in standard Russian; e.g. "I haven't eaten today" will be "Я сегодня не евши" instead of "Я сегодня не ел".

sourceUrl=https://ru.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Вечера_у_княгини_Старобельской_(Чернышевский)&oldid=5336197
sourceRevision=5336197
sourceRecordId=vechera:устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и уры
originalSourceText=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и урывками между этих занятий услышал утром голос старушки, хозяйки домика: «самовар принесла, Павел Сергеевич», сказал ей «благодарю, Анна Даниловна», выпил два стакана чаю с молоком, съел с чаем ломоть белого хлеба; опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла завтрак, Павел Сергеевич», опять поблагодарил ее, выпил стакан молока с черным хлебом, составлявший его завтрак, опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла обед, Павел Сергеич», опять поблагодарил ее, съел тарелку грешневой кашицы, составлявшую его ежедневный обед; опять услышал — это было, наверное часов в 6, по аккуратно соблюдаемому старушкой порядку, — голос ее «принесла самовар, Павел Сергеич!», опять сказал «благодарю вас, Анна Даниловна», — но тут произошло отступление от ежедневного порядка: встав положить чаю в чайник, и налить кипятком, Павел Сергеич увидел, что старушка не ушла, стоит, сложа руки
shippedText=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая
transformation=selection
licenseId=Public domain
licenseUrl=https://ru.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Викитека:Авторские_права&oldid=5723825
attribution=Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский. Вечера у княгини Старобельской, published 1888. Russian Wikisource oldid 5336197 records the work with PD-old. Шаблон:PD-old oldid 3794015 redirects to Шаблон:PD-old-70 oldid 5691096, which states that the work entered the public domain in Russia under Civil Code article 1281 and in countries whose term is the author's life plus 70 years. Автор:Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский oldid 5696210 records the death year 1889. Шаблон:АП oldid 4965505 defines that year parameter as the author's year of death. Викитека:Авторские права oldid 5723825 is the project license snapshot. Project hosting is not the public-domain basis. Retrieved 2026-09-28T16:43:13Z.

originalSourceText=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и урывками между этих занятий услышал утром голос старушки, хозяйки домика: «самовар принесла, Павел Сергеевич», сказал ей «благодарю, Анна Даниловна», выпил два стакана чаю с молоком, съел с чаем ломоть белого хлеба; опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла завтрак, Павел Сергеевич», опять поблагодарил ее, выпил стакан молока с черным хлебом, составлявший его завтрак, опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла обед, Павел Сергеич», опять поблагодарил ее, съел тарелку грешневой кашицы, составлявшую его ежедневный обед; опять услышал — это было, наверное часов в 6, по аккуратно соблюдаемому старушкой порядку, — голос ее «принесла самовар, Павел Сергеич!», опять сказал «благодарю вас, Анна Даниловна», — но тут произошло отступление от ежедневного порядка: встав положить чаю в чайник, и налить кипятком, Павел Сергеич увидел, что старушка не ушла, стоит, сложа руки
shippedText=диван
transformation=selection
licenseId=Public domain
licenseUrl=https://ru.wikisource.org/w/index.php?title=Викитека:Авторские_права&oldid=5723825
attribution=Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский. Вечера у княгини Старобельской, published 1888. Russian Wikisource oldid 5336197 records the work with PD-old. Шаблон:PD-old oldid 3794015 redirects to Шаблон:PD-old-70 oldid 5691096, which states that the work entered the public domain in Russia under Civil Code article 1281 and in countries whose term is the author's life plus 70 years. Автор:Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский oldid 5696210 records the death year 1889. Шаблон:АП oldid 4965505 defines that year parameter as the author's year of death. Викитека:Авторские права oldid 5723825 is the project license snapshot. Project hosting is not the public-domain basis. Retrieved 2026-09-28T16:43:13Z.
shareAlikeTreatment=The retained literary span is in the public domain under the recorded life-plus-70 basis. Project hosting is not that basis. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside this statement. No audio is included.

sourceId=src-content-audit-research
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-corrected-capability:cap-obj-u28-imperfective-adverb
sourceHash=3ec1b25855c9c37e0dd1ad494c532fa84e6a7f646a0b79fa3da4c58d578ff3f0
supportedClaim=imperfective-verbal-adverb
exactSpan=Pending corrected research applications. No linguistic or owner approval is recorded.
References: retained original source bindings in this unit; the independent content-audit register; Gramota dictionary and orthography; Litnevskaya, verbal adverbs; Rusgram, verbal adverbs.
https://gramota.ru/poisk?query=быстрый&mode=slovari
https://gramota.ru/biblioteka/spravochniki/russkij-yazyk-kratkij-teoreticheskij-kurs-dlya-shkolnikov/deeprichastie
Proposed Unit 28 uses admitted imperfective and perfective adverbial lines and one admitted public-domain reading span. Qualified review is pending.
Read the admitted examples on this card.
An imperfective verbal adverb is simultaneous with the main predicate. It has no independent tense, so it is not usually present as an absolute label. The subject is the same as the main verb.
imperfective ones are usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
думая
imperfective verbal adverb; relative time is interpreted with its main predicate, normally simultaneous in the taught construction
учась
читая письмо, он улыбался
simultaneous with the main predicate
Which English description identifies the timing of читая in this sentence?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, simultaneous with the main predicate describes читая: he reads while smiling. The past main verb does not turn читая into an absolute present tense.
In читая письмо, он улыбался, читая is simultaneous with the main predicate: the same person reads and smiles during the same interval.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label ignores the past main verb; the verbal adverb has relative time.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject condition in this construction.
the reader and the person smiling are the same person
Which English description identifies the shared subject?
In читая письмо, он улыбался, the reader and the person smiling are the same person: the verbal adverb shares the subject of the main predicate.
two unrelated people perform the actions
the sentence omits its main subject
two unrelated people perform the actions is not the shared-subject construction shown here.
the sentence omits its main subject is incorrect because the displayed main clause has an explicit subject.

sourceHash=29472cae0668a27d911dc90458ef77b06d145c822d0f3f944666ebdd91b0c8e7
supportedClaim=perfective-verbal-adverb
exactSpan=Pending corrected research applications. No linguistic or owner approval is recorded.
References: retained original source bindings in this unit; the independent content-audit register; Gramota dictionary and orthography; Litnevskaya, verbal adverbs; Rusgram, verbal adverbs.
https://gramota.ru/poisk?query=быстрый&mode=slovari
https://gramota.ru/biblioteka/spravochniki/russkij-yazyk-kratkij-teoreticheskij-kurs-dlya-shkolnikov/deeprichastie
Proposed Unit 28 uses admitted imperfective and perfective adverbial lines and one admitted public-domain reading span. Qualified review is pending.
Read the admitted examples on this card.
A perfective verbal adverb is anterior to the main predicate. It can precede a past or a future main action. It is not limited to an absolute past.
usually present, while perfective ones can only be past
сказав
perfective verbal adverb; normally anterior to its main action, which can itself be past or future
научившись
войдя
написав письмо, она отправит его завтра
anterior to a future main action
When is the perfective verbal adverb interpreted in this sentence?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, anterior to a future main action describes написав: writing precedes the future sending, and the same person performs both actions.
an absolute present label
a different subject from the main verb
an absolute present label is not the time of a perfective verbal adverb.
a different subject from the main verb violates the shared-subject constraint.
the writer and sender are the same person
Which English description identifies the shared subject?
In написав письмо, она отправит его завтра, the writer and sender are the same person: the verbal adverb shares the subject of the main predicate.
two unrelated people perform the actions
the sentence omits its main subject
two unrelated people perform the actions is not the shared-subject construction shown here.
the sentence omits its main subject is incorrect because the displayed main clause has an explicit subject.

sourceRecordId=vechera:устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и уры
sourceHash=b32e8b54142118e2c3b0c06955b9c84ced8c8abe48cfc73facba4ff8eda261c3
supportedClaim=verbal-adverb-reading
exactSpan=устав писать ложился на диван и отдыхал читая, потом опять садился писать, и урывками между этих занятий услышал утром голос старушки, хозяйки домика: «самовар принесла, Павел Сергеевич», сказал ей «благодарю, Анна Даниловна», выпил два стакана чаю с молоком, съел с чаем ломоть белого хлеба; опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла завтрак, Павел Сергеевич», опять поблагодарил ее, выпил стакан молока с черным хлебом, составлявший его завтрак, опять услышал голос хозяйки: «принесла обед, Павел Сергеич», опять поблагодарил ее, съел тарелку грешневой кашицы, составлявшую его ежедневный обед; опять услышал — это было, наверное часов в 6, по аккуратно соблюдаемому старушкой порядку, — голос ее «принесла самовар, Павел Сергеич!», опять сказал «благодарю вас, Анна Даниловна», — но тут произошло отступление от ежедневного порядка: встав положить чаю в чайник, и налить кипятком, Павел Сергеич увидел, что старушка не ушла, стоит, сложа руки

Вечера у княгини Старобельской (Чернышевский)
Public domain
Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский. Вечера у княгини Старобельской, published 1888. Russian Wikisource oldid 5336197 records the work with PD-old. Шаблон:PD-old oldid 3794015 redirects to Шаблон:PD-old-70 oldid 5691096, which states that the work entered the public domain in Russia under Civil Code article 1281 and in countries whose term is the author's life plus 70 years. Автор:Николай Гаврилович Чернышевский oldid 5696210 records the death year 1889. Шаблон:АП oldid 4965505 defines that year parameter as the author's year of death. Викитека:Авторские права oldid 5723825 is the project license snapshot. Project hosting is not the public-domain basis. Retrieved 2026-09-28T16:43:13Z.
Selected spans only. Russian quotations are unchanged. English claims are quoted, not paraphrased.
